The analysts wrote in a note that the event reinforced a clear message that AI infrastructure demand continues to outstrip supply, supporting a constructive view on hyperscalers into upcoming earnings. \u201cOverall, we continue to be incrementally more positive on AI demand vs our checks in early April,\u201d they wrote. Ahead of first quarter financial results, investors are focused on cloud growth, backlog trends, and any changes to capital expenditure or capacity plans. Jefferies said it is incrementally more positive on hyperscalers, with the strongest cloud upside skew seen at Google Cloud, followed by AWS and Azure. Jefferies highlighted elevated expectations for AWS, though the firm maintained a high conviction view on Amazon. Microsoft is increasingly being assessed on Microsoft 365 and Copilot traction rather than Azure beat size, while Alphabet remains positive on trajectory but may be constrained by valuation. A major theme was continued AI compute scarcity, with tight supply across GPU generations including Hopper and Blackwell, alongside limited spot availability for older chips. Industry feedback suggested a growing shift toward committed capacity contracts as hyperscalers lock in supply amid persistent shortages. Capital expenditure across cloud providers is also expected"

    "content": "Core Scientific seeks $3.3 billion bond sale to further AI data center pivot Search / News Video Prices Research Consensus 2026 Data & Indices Sponsored Search / en Finance Share Share this article Copy link X icon X (Twitter) LinkedIn Facebook Email Core Scientific seeks $3.3 billion bond sale to further AI data center pivot The company is building six AI data centers, leased to CoreWeave for 12 years, and is expected to generate approximately $10 billion in revenue. By Francisco Rodrigues | Edited by Aoyon Ashraf Apr 21, 2026, 7:27 p.m. Make preferred on What to know : Core Scientific (CORZ) is raising $3.3B via a junk bond sale to finance its shift from crypto mining to AI-focused data center operations. The company is building six AI data centers, leased to CoreWeave for 12 years, and is expected to generate approximately $10 billion in revenue. This high-yield debt issuance (part of $17.9B raised in AI infrastructure this year) will fund construction costs, repay existing debt, and build reserves. Core Scientific (CORZ) is preparing to raise $3.3 billion through a junk bond sale as it continues its transition toward artificial intelligence-focused data center operations. Demand for AI services has pushed data centers, power supply and advanced chips to their limits. To keep up, firms are tapping riskier parts of the debt market for funds to keep developing their operations. Core Scientific, once a bitcoin miner, sold $175 million in bitcoin last month to further its AI pivot. Borrowers linked to AI infrastructure have raised $17.9 billion in junk bonds so far this year, Bloomberg reported . CORZ itself is building six data centers that will support AI workloads, with the capacity leased to CoreWeave under a 12-year agreement that could bring in around $10 billion in revenue, the report adds, citing sources familiar with the deal. Core Scientific\u2019s move follows a string of large deals. Recent offerings tied to Google-backed data centers and CoreWeave raised a combined $6.7 billion. Another firm, Edged Compute, is marketing $1.3 billion in bonds to fund facilities leased to CoreWeave and an Alibaba unit. Core Scientific said it will use proceeds to repay existing debt and fund reserves. It also plans to support construction across several states if costs exceed available funds, signaling how capital-intensive the AI buildout has become. The company still holds \u201cunder 1,000 bitcoin,\u201d according to CFO Jim Nygaard. Big AI pivot Core Scientific was founded in 2017 and grew into one of North America's largest bitcoin miners before filing for Chapter 11 in December 2022, squeezed by high power costs and a weak bitcoin price. It emerged from reorganization in January 2024 and was relisted on Nasdaq under the ticker CORZ. The pivot from bitcoin mining to AI hosting is all about the margins. The April 2024 halving cut block rewards from 6.25 BTC to 3.125, and by late 2025, the average cash cost to mine one bitcoin rose while the price of BTC itself had been on a downturn, from"

Published April 22, 2026 Makenzie Holland Senior News Writer Share Copy link Email LinkedIn X/Twitter Facebook Print License Add us on Google The CoreWeave name and logo displayed on billboards in Times Square on March 28, 2025, in NYC. CoreWeave is collaborating with Google Cloud to ease cross-cloud interconnectivity. Michael M. Santiago / Staff via Getty Images Listen to the article 3 min This audio is auto-generated. Please let us know if you have feedback . Dive Brief: CoreWeave is launching a trio of new capabilities aimed at simplifying cross-cloud AI training and inference, according to a Wednesday announcement. The new features include CoreWeave Interconnect, which directly links the AI cloud provider to Google Cloud and will be available soon in private preview for select regions. CoreWeave Interconnect uses Google Cloud\u2019s Partner Cross-Cloud Interconnect capability, which allows developers to run training and inference workloads where needed. The interconnectivity makes it easier for developers to build distributed AI-powered services, said Rob Enns, VP and general manager of cloud networking at Google Cloud, in the announcement. CoreWeave also launched SUNK Anywhere and LOTA Cross-Cloud. SUNK Anywhere supports large-scale, long-running AI training projects, allowing developers to add capacity acros s environments including Google Cloud, AWS, Microsoft Azure and on-premises. Meanwhile, LOTA Cross-Cloud gives AI builders the ability to store data in one place while running workloads across cloud environments. Dive Insight: CoreWeave\u2019s addition of cross-cloud capabilities supporting AI workloads comes as hyperscalers push to harness open cloud environments for enterprises deploying the technology at scale. In December, AWS and Google Cloud launched a joint multicloud collaboration creating an open specification for network interoperability and enabling customers to shift from manually connecting clouds to deploying cloud-native experiences. Microsoft Azure is set to join the collaboration later in 2026. \u201cWe are excited about this collaboration which enables our customers to move their data and applications between clouds with simplified global connectivity and enhanced operational effectiveness,\u201d Enns said in a press release at the time. While it spurred connectivity across multicloud environments, the collaboration also put pressure on other cloud providers to adopt Google Cloud and AWS\u2019 interoperability standard, Forrester Principal Analyst Lee Sustar told CIO Dive when the partnership"

In this article CRWV Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT watch now VIDEO 3:17 03:17 CoreWeave CEO Intrator on company's debt load: 'Scaling is expensive' Squawk on the Street CoreWeave announced Friday that it has entered a multi-year agreement with Anthropic to power its Claude artificial intelligence models as demand for infrastructure continues to climb. Shares of the neocloud climbed 11% following the news. \"With the addition of Anthropic, nine of the leading ten AI model providers now leverage CoreWeave's platform, reflecting the growing demand for infrastructure that can support AI at scale,\" CoreWeave said in the announcement. The deal comes one day after Meta committed to spending an additional $21 billion with the AI cloud provider. The deal adds to a prior $14.2 billion commitment announced in September. CoreWeave declined to disclose the value of the Anthropic deal. The cloud provider told CNBC that of the top ten foundational AI models, Elon Musk 's xAI is the one company they do not yet work with.     "content": "Meta commits $21B to CoreWeave, signaling neoclouds as core AI infrastructure layer | Edge Infrastructure Review AI Infrastructure Cloud Platforms Connectivity Data Centers Edge Computing rss linkedin facebook search search --> About News Companies Markets Directory Guides Resources Events Subscribe Search for: Search X Share X/Tweet Link Comment Meta commits $21B to CoreWeave, signaling neoclouds as core AI infrastructure layer Apr 23, 2026 | Stephen Mayhew Categories Digital Infrastructure News | Technology & Architecture Neocloud provider, CoreWeave and Meta announced a $21 billion agreement to expand AI infrastructure, providing AI cloud capacity through December 2032. Meta is using its AI development and deployment alongside CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud platform in partnership. The infrastructure will be distributed across multiple locations, including those located in deployments of NVIDIA Vera Rubin, to provide performance, resiliency and scalability. \u201cThis is another example that leading companies are choosing CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud to run their most demanding workloads,\u201d says Michael Intrator, Co-founder, CEO, Chairman of CoreWeave. The deal underscores the increasing need for high-performance infrastructure in order to support demanding AI workloads at any scale. It also validates neoclouds as a critical layer in AI infrastructure. It highlights hyperscalers increasingly relying on external GPU-centric platforms to scale faster, manage supply constraints, and support distributed AI workloads, reinforcing the shift toward high-density, performance-optimized AI factory architectures. CoreWeave is an AI cloud powerhouse provider used by the largest AI labs, startups and enterprises.The company continued to grow its services and partnerships centered on AI, eventually going public on the Nasdaq in March 2025. The stock moved higher after Nvidia (NASDAQ:NVDA) 's $2 billion investment and a Deutsche Bank upgrade. Investors are watching CoreWeave\u2019s AI data center buildout toward 5\u20137.9 GW of capacity by 2030. CoreWeave\u2019s trading volume reached 45.4 million shares, coming in about 55% above compared with its three-month average of 29.3 million shares. CoreWeave IPO'd in 2025 and has grown 172% since going public. How the markets moved today The S&P 500 (SNPINDEX:^GSPC) added 0.41% to finish Tuesday at 6,979, while the Nasdaq Composite (NASDAQINDEX:^IXIC) rose 0.91% to close at 23,817. Within cloud infrastructure services, industry peers Nvidia (NASDAQ:NVDA) closed at $188.52 (+1.10%) and Microsoft (NASDAQ:MSFT) finished at $480.58 (+2.19%) as investors tracked AI-focused capacity and spending plans. What this means for investors CoreWeave rose sharply on Tuesday after Nvidia committed $2 billion to the AI infrastructure provider, a move that investors read as a strong endorsement of CoreWeave\u2019s position in the fast-growing market for GPU-powered cloud services. The investment, priced at $87.20 a share, deepens ties between the chipmaker and CoreWeave\u2019s data center platform. Reports that Nvidia\u2019s stake could increase to roughly 11% and help fund plans for more than 5 gigawatts of AI data center capacity by 2030 added to the rally. The stock also benefited from a Deutsche Bank upgrade, which brought added institutional attention to the story.     "published": "2026-04-09T07:00:00Z",
    "content": "CoreWeave Takes As Much Financial Engineering As It Does Datacenter Design Jump to main content Search More topics Financial results IT spending Infrastructure Anthropic AMD Nvidia Google Microsoft Meta Platforms AWS OpenAI Database All the latest news, from all sections Search Compute Store Connect Control Code HPC AI Hyperscale Cloud Edge Enterprise NEXTPLATFORM AD CLOUD CoreWeave Takes As Much Financial Engineering As It Does Datacenter Design Timothy Prickett Morgan Timothy Prickett Morgan Co-Editor, Co-Founder, The Next Platform Published thu 9 Apr 2026 // 21:57 UTC Meta Platforms has aspirations to be one of the big AI model builders and to push the state of the art to \u201csuperintelligence,\u201d under the impression that it can sell access to personal AI agents to its more than 1 billion users worldwide. It is a reasonable business model \u2013 and a lot more sensible than the metaverse that was the obsession of company founder Mark Zuckerberg a few years and a few tens of billions of dollars ago. While the social network and AI model maker has a fondness for its own server, storage, switch, and datacenter designs, even after the launch of the Open Compute Project and its open strategy firmly charted, the company has not been shy about renting datacenter capacity when and where it needed to. And in the GenAI boom, Meta Platforms has not shied away from renting compute capacity from others. NEXTPLATFORM AD Back in September 2025, Meta Platforms inked a supplier deal with king of the neoclouds, CoreWeave, that saw the former buy $14.2 billion in AI processing capacity through December 2031 . This was a big deal for CoreWeave, which had just gone public and which needed to show that it has customers other than Microsoft renting its GPU hours. With the deal announced today in an 8-K filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission , CoreWeave said that Meta Platforms has signed up for an additional $21 billion in AI processing capacity running out through December 2032. No one knows the ramp of that capacity usage, and it is no doubt nailed down pretty hard because the one thing CoreWeave can\u2019t have is a GPU burning capital and electricity and a customer not paying for the privilege of using it. It is not safe to assume that all $21 billion of spending happens in 2032, the incremental year, but for all we know that is exactly what will happen. This deal will bring CoreWeave\u2019s revenue backlog up to $87.8 billion, and Meta Platforms will represent 40.1 percent of that backlog. Back in March 2025 , OpenAI inked a five year deal with CoreWeave for $11.9 billion in capacity, and another $10.5 billion in capacity was added last year, bringing the total to $22.4 billion in capacity. Assuming that none of that OpenAI capacity has been activated as yet, that would represent 25.5 percent of that extended backlog. So these two customers \u2013 Meta Platforms and OpenAI \u2013 represent nearly two-thirds of the guaranteed revenue on"

Christine Queally \u2022 Apr 19, 2026, 10:20 PM \u2022 Updated Share: Top Stories 01:38 Would you pay more than $2M to see the FIFA World Cup final in East Rutherford? 02:28 Soggy, cool temperatures spoil Saturday with clouds and emerging sun on Sunday Crews work to repair large sinkhole on busy Newark street 01:57 Westfield mom documents journey with children with autism Popular radio host Big Joe Henry nominated for NJ Hall of Fame 00:21 Galloway woman sentenced to 5 years in prison for drugs, weapons possession 01:56 Why it's cheaper for NJ soccer fans to go to FIFA World Cup in Philadelphia instead of East Rutherford 00:27 Edison stabbing suspect captured in Ohio A $1.8 billion artificial intelligence data center under construction in Kenilworth is facing pushback from residents. The project is located at the Northeast Science and Technology Center (NEST) campus. The old Merck & Co. building will house the data center. CoreWeave, a company that specializes in AI infrastructure, purchased a large portion of property on the campus in 2025 to build the center. Despite the Kenilworth Borough Council's approval of the project, it has largely flown under the radar until recently. \"Social media, that's the only way you'll hear about it here even though we're only 200 yards from the location,\" said Union County resident Brent Krueger. Some residents told News 12 they're concerned about having a data center built near their homes. They mentioned concerns about water quality, flood risk and a potential increase in their electric bill. \"We're going to have major issues with the water in the future, with so many data centers being built. It's just not possible. We cannot sustain it. They're destroying the earth,\" said Union County resident Rosa Joseph. Neighbors even circulated a petition to stop the construction, with over 1,500 signatures as of Sunday night. \"Hopefully, this can happen. We still have time,\" Joseph said. Kenilworth Mayor Linda Karlovitch, however, said residents shouldn't see much of a difference, if any at all, once the center is operational. \"CoreWeave will be paying sales tax on the power, funding all required transmission upgrades. Those costs will not be passed on to residential rate payers,\" she said. \"I want people to know they're using a closed loop cooling system and it avoids continuous use of local water.\" Karlovitch told News 12 the borough is expecting significant economic payoff from CoreWeave's investment, including over 140 jobs. \"When Merck left, it left a real gap in jobs, in tax"

Search Blog Markets Non Sorted Data Processing Servers Apr 19, 2026 AI Infrastructure Boom: Neocloud Providers CoreWeave and Nebius Report Surging Revenue According to a recent report from Yahoo Finance, companies providing artificial intelligence infrastructure are currently experiencing significant growth and profitability. This sector is highlighted as a high-growth area for investment. Neocloud Providers Showcase Rapid Expansion Two notable companies in this space, CoreWeave and Nebius , are classified as neocloud providers, meaning they specialize in delivering AI-specific computing power. While both secure major contracts from large technology firms, their service models differ. CoreWeave concentrates primarily on providing graphics processing units, whereas Nebius offers a more comprehensive, full-stack solution for AI development. Financial Performance and Projections Nebius reported a substantial year-over-year revenue increase in the fourth quarter, reaching a figure of $228 million. The company anticipates its annual revenue run rate will grow significantly by the end of 2026. CoreWeave also demonstrated strong growth, with its fourth-quarter revenue rising year-over-year to $1.6 billion. CoreWeave holds a considerable backlog and expects to recognize a specific revenue total over the next two years. Another company mentioned alongside these firms is Applied Digital , which is also noted for its high growth rate within the AI infrastructure industry. For Journalists - CoreWeave and Meta Announce $21 Billion Expanded AI Infrastructure Agreement Share Meta to leverage CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud platform to scale inference workloads, underscoring the surging demand for large-scale AI compute LIVINGSTON, N.J.--( BUSINESS WIRE )-- CoreWeave (Nasdaq: CRWV), the Essential Cloud for AI\u2122, today announced an expanded, long-term agreement with Meta Platforms, Inc. to provide AI cloud capacity through December 2032 for approximately $21 billion. With this deal, the two companies are continuing their existing relationship, increasing support for Meta\u2019s development and deployment of AI. The dedicated capacity will be deployed across multiple locations and will include some of the initial deployments of the NVIDIA Vera Rubin platform. This distributed approach is designed to optimize performance, resilience, and scalability for Meta\u2019s AI operations. The new agreement is a clear signal of the industry\u2019s accelerating demand for high-performance infrastructure capable of supporting increasingly complex, large-scale AI workloads. \u201cThis is another example that leading companies are choosing CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud to run their most demanding workloads,\u201d said Michael Intrator, Co-founder, CEO, Chairman of CoreWeave. Additional details regarding the agreement are available in CoreWeave\u2019s filing with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission. About CoreWeave CoreWeave is The Essential Cloud for AI\u2122. Built for pioneers by pioneers, CoreWeave delivers a platform of technology, tools, and teams that enables innovators to move at the pace of innovation, building and scaling AI with confidence. Trusted by leading AI labs, startups, and global enterprises, CoreWeave serves as a force multiplier by combining superior infrastructure performance with deep technical expertise to accelerate breakthroughs. Established in 2017, CoreWeave completed its public listing on Nasdaq (CRWV) in March 2025. Learn more at www.coreweave.com .     "content": "Tens of billions in days: CoreWeave shows how aggressively AI infrastructure is being funded | Fortune Search Subscribe Home Latest Fortune 500 Finance Tech Leadership Lifestyle Rankings Multimedia Newsletters CFO Daily Tens of billions in days: CoreWeave shows how aggressively AI infrastructure is being funded By Sheryl Estrada Sheryl Estrada Senior Writer and author of CFO Daily Down Arrow Button Icon By Sheryl Estrada Sheryl Estrada Senior Writer and author of CFO Daily Down Arrow Button Icon April 13, 2026, 6:55 AM ET Add us on Getty Images Good morning. For finance leaders trying to understand how the AI infrastructure race is being funded, CoreWeave just offered a revealing case study. Recommended Video In the span of a few days, the Nvidia-backed startup locked in tens of billions in customer commitments and layered on multiple forms of debt. This underscores a defining feature of today\u2019s AI boom: growth is being financed as aggressively as it is being engineered. CoreWeave, which provides cloud-based GPUs powered by Nvidia chips, announced on Thursday a $21 billion deal with Meta running from 2027 to 2032. That brings Meta\u2019s total commitment to more than $35 billion, which could be viewed as a significant vote of confidence in sustained demand for AI compute. At the same time, the company raised roughly $3.5 billion in convertible senior notes, a hybrid instrument that blends debt with an equity upside. Investors collect interest, but can convert into shares if CoreWeave\u2019s valuation rises. It\u2019s a structure that limits near-term cash strain while effectively betting that future equity will be worth more. And that wasn\u2019t all. CoreWeave CFO Nitin Agrawal said in a LinkedIn post on Friday that the company also: \u2014Upsized a high-yield bond offering due to heavy demand. \u2014Secured an $8.5 billion delayed draw term loan at investment-grade ratings. \u2014Executed what it described as one of the largest dual-tranche raises of its kind. Also on Friday, the company\u2019s stock climbed as much as 13% after it announced a multi-year agreement with Anthropic . Taken together, it shows that capital is flowing freely to AI infrastructure\u2014at least for now. I asked Morningstar equity analyst Luke Yang what this shows for how AI-native companies are approaching capital formation right now. He pointed to three aspects: \u2014Cloud infrastructure companies will continue to use all kinds of tools available to get the funds necessary for capacity expansion. \u201cFor this Meta-CoreWeave deal specifically, we see the company leveraging delayed draw term loans, corporate bonds, and convertible notes. Neoclouds also use OEM financing, operating/financing leases, share issuance, etc. Growth is the top priority, and financing should not be a bottleneck for these companies\u2019 growth.\u201d \u2014Creditors are getting more comfortable with the business of neoclouds. \u201cWe see a sequential improvement in the interest rates and credit ratings of CoreWeave\u2019s delayed draw term loans. The DDTL 4.0 facility is the first investment-grade facility with around a 6% interest rate. Going forward, we expect to see more neocloud companies securing investment-grade financing with take-or-pay deals from credible AI"

Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 19, 2026 Five AI Stocks Under $200 for Investment in 2026 A recent article from Yahoo Finance notes that while fractional shares are increasingly available, some investors still face barriers to purchasing high-priced stocks, making reasonably priced full shares a component of certain investment approaches. The source identifies five companies considered worthy purchases for investors limited to a $200 per share budget, ranging from established entities to emerging businesses. Nvidia Nvidia stock trades near $201 per share. The company produces GPUs, which have become a primary computing unit since a competitive surge in artificial intelligence began in 2023. Wall Street analysts forecast revenue growth of 79% for the first quarter and 85% for the second quarter. Significant planned spending on AI infrastructure by large-scale operators is viewed as a positive indicator for the company's future. Nebius Nebius stock has nearly doubled in value this year. The company provides AI-focused computing hardware to clients, a focus driving notable growth. Analysts project revenue growth of 522% for this year and 195% for the following year, which represents the fastest growth rate among the listed stocks. These projections suggest potential for further stock price appreciation. CoreWeave CoreWeave operates in a comparable sector to Nebius, focusing on GPU-based cloud computing infrastructure. While its growth rate is lower, its overall scale is larger. At the end of 2027, Wall Street expects $23.3 billion in revenue for CoreWeave versus Nebius's $9.7 billion. Projected growth rates of 142% for 2026 and 87% for 2027 indicate strong analyst confidence in the company's products. The source concludes that both firms appear to be solid investment choices currently. 1.     "content": "IREN Stock Faces May 7 Test as AI Data Center Pivot, Microsoft Deal and $6 Billion Share-Sale Risk Collide Dark Light Today: 24 April 2026 Subscribe TechStock\u00b2 Real-time market monitoring Home Stock Market Earnings Metals Crypto AI Technology About Us Contact TechStock\u00b2 24 April 2026 Artificial Intelligence \u00b7 NASDAQ:IREN \u00b7 NASDAQ:MSFT \u00b7 NASDAQ:NVDA \u00b7 Stock Market \u00b7 Technology 3 mins read IREN Stock Faces May 7 Test as AI Data Center Pivot, Microsoft Deal and $6 Billion Share-Sale Risk Collide by Khadija Saeed New York, April 24, 2026, 07:56 EDT IREN Limited has locked in May 7 for its fiscal Q3 earnings update, teeing up the next big look at its AI data center push after shares on the Nasdaq surged 7.5% Thursday. The company plans to detail results for the quarter through March 31, with a call for investors scheduled at 5 p.m. Eastern. Timing is key here: IREN isn\u2019t really seen as just another bitcoin miner anymore. What investors want now? Evidence that its power plants, GPUs \u2014 those chips at the heart of AI training and inference \u2014 and hefty customer deals can actually bring in cash quickly enough to pay for all this expensive expansion. The question is now harder to ignore for the stock. IREN climbed $3.63 to finish Thursday at $52.02, bucking a broader market pullback as the Nasdaq Composite slipped 0.9%. The pop, unusual on a sluggish day, points to traders taking another look at IREN\u2019s data center strategy. May\u2019s numbers are set to land after a bumpy December quarter for IREN. Revenue in the fiscal second quarter dropped to $184.7 million, down from $240.3 million the quarter before. The company posted a net loss of $155.4 million, reversing from net income of $384.6 million. Bitcoin mining accounted for $167.4 million\u2014still dwarfing AI Cloud Services, which pulled in $17.3 million. But that AI revenue more than doubled compared to the September quarter. Management has been steering the conversation toward the ramp. Back in February, Daniel Roberts\u2014IREN\u2019s co-founder and co-CEO\u2014described the current market as the \u201cstrongest demand environment to date.\u201d IREN has also maintained that its 140,000-GPU buildout remains on schedule, targeting $3.4 billion in annualized run-rate revenue. That figure reflects management\u2019s projection for yearly revenue once all new capacity comes online, not revenue already in the books. GlobeNewswire The target got bigger in March. IREN announced a deal to acquire over 50,000 Nvidia B300 GPUs, which would push its planned total to 150,000 GPUs. The company\u2019s expecting to roll out the expanded fleet in stages during the second half of 2026. By the end of that year, IREN projects the operation could generate more than $3.7 billion in annualized run-rate revenue \u2014 although it warned the figure isn\u2019t fully contracted and could end up looking quite different. Microsoft remains the linchpin. Back in November, IREN announced a five-year GPU cloud services deal with Microsoft worth roughly $9.7 billion, which included a 20% prepayment. There\u2019s also a separate $5.8 billion arrangement with Dell Technologies"

Both stocks have pulled back sharply from their 2025 highs. Wall Street\u2019s rapidly expanding analyst coverage of both names suggests institutional conviction is building, though Nebius commands a higher valuation multiple and a stronger margin outlook. Now Live: Discover how much upside your favorite stocks could have using TIKR\u2019s new Valuation Model (It\u2019s free) >>> Two artificial intelligence infrastructure plays are grabbing investor attention right now. Both are building the data centers that power the AI boom. Both are early-stage, burning cash, and priced on future potential rather than today\u2019s profits. But Wall Street doesn\u2019t treat them equally \u2014 and the gap is wider than you might think. Nebius Group ( NBIS ) and Iren Limited ( IREN ) are chasing the same tailwind: surging demand for GPU compute. The question is which one has stronger analyst conviction. The numbers tell a clear story. Estimate a company\u2019s fair value instantly (Free with TIKR) >>> Why traditional earnings metrics don\u2019t work here Before comparing the two, it helps to understand what each company does. Nebius is the Amsterdam-listed rebuild of Yandex\u2019s core AI and cloud infrastructure business. After geopolitical events forced its founding team out of Russia in 2022, chief executive Arkady Volozh and hundreds of engineers relocated to Europe and restarted from scratch. Today, Nebius operates a full-stack AI cloud \u2014 think GPU clusters, managed storage, and developer tools \u2014 targeting AI start-ups and enterprises that need compute but can\u2019t afford to build their own. At a Morgan Stanley conference in March 2026, Volozh described the ambition plainly: the company is targeting nearly one gigawatt of active power this year and has a pipeline reserved for up to three gigawatts. \u201cDemand far exceeds our supply,\u201d Nebius Chief Revenue Officer Marc Boroditsky told the audience, according to the conference transcript. Iren is an Australian-founded company that spent seven years as a Bitcoin miner before pivoting hard into AI cloud hosting. It now operates 810 megawatts of existing data centers \u2014 predominantly air-cooled, which is increasingly in demand \u2014 and has secured a $9.7 billion AI contract with Microsoft. On Iren\u2019s Feb. 5 earnings call, Co-CEO Daniel Roberts said the company is on track to deliver 140,000 GPUs by the end of 2026, targeting $3.4 billion in"

    "content": "Why IREN stock could be massively undervalued right now News Entertainment Culture Business Health IQ Beauty Videos News Entertainment Culture Business Health IQ Beauty Videos Close Open Search Search [wpcode id=\"1725568\"] Home \u00bb Business Why IREN stock could be massively undervalued right now Shares are trading nearly 50% below analyst targets after a strong monthly run By Tega Egwabor Apr 23, 2026 11:18 am Photo credit: shutterstock/PJ McDonnell IREN has been quietly putting together one of the more intriguing stock stories in the cryptocurrency and blockchain space, and a growing number of investors are starting to pay attention. Shares climbed 7.1% in a single trading session recently, adding to a 14.8% gain over the past month. Over the past year, the stock has returned approximately 7%. With shares currently trading at $48.39, those numbers suggest momentum is building \u2014 yet the most striking detail about IREN right now is not where the stock has been, but how far some analysts believe it still has to go. A valuation gap that\u2019s hard to ignore According to analysis from Simply Wall St, IREN\u2019s estimated fair value sits at approximately $95.75 \u2014 nearly double its current trading price. That means shares are changing hands at roughly 49% below what the model suggests the company is actually worth, a gap wide enough to turn heads even among cautious investors. The methodology behind that figure involves a discounted cash flow analysis built around a long-term growth scenario. The projection assumes IREN could generate revenue of $8.7 billion and earnings of $2.9 billion by 2031, implying an annual growth rate of 63%. The model applies a 10% discount rate, a 33% profit margin and a future price-to-earnings multiple of 25 times \u2014 figures broadly in line with valuations seen among established software companies. Central to that optimistic outlook is a significant expansion in Bitcoin-related data center revenue, paired with stable profit margins over time. The company has already demonstrated its ability to attract major partners, having secured an AI cloud deal with Microsoft that helped fuel recent share price momentum and reinforced the investment case for those watching the stock closely. What could get in the way Despite the compelling valuation argument, there are real risks that investors need to weigh carefully before drawing any conclusions about IREN \u2018s long-term trajectory. The 2 most prominent concerns are: 1. the potential for future dilution through capital raising activity, which could reduce the value of existing shares as the company funds its growth ambitions, and 2. the inherent volatility of Bitcoin prices, which directly affects the economics of IREN\u2019s mining operations. A sustained drop in Bitcoin\u2019s value would put meaningful pressure on revenue projections and potentially widen the gap between current results and what the long-term model assumes. Those risks help explain why market sentiment around IREN remains genuinely mixed. The enthusiasm is real \u2014 a 7.1% single-day gain and a double-digit monthly return are not figures that go unnoticed. But the caution is equally real, particularly among"

    "content": "IREN Stock Jumps as AI Cloud Bet Meets $6 Billion Dilution Test Dark Light Today: 24 April 2026 Subscribe TechStock\u00b2 Real-time market monitoring Home Stock Market Earnings Metals Crypto AI Technology About Us Contact TechStock\u00b2 23 April 2026 Artificial Intelligence \u00b7 Cloud Computing \u00b7 NYSE:IREN \u00b7 Stock Market 3 mins read IREN Stock Jumps as AI Cloud Bet Meets $6 Billion Dilution Test by Khadija Saeed New York, April 23, 2026, 12:01 EDT IREN jumped 7.3% to $51.94 by late morning in New York on Thursday, thrusting the ex-bitcoin miner into a renewed debate: can its AI cloud push really outpace the $6 billion financing overhang dogging the stock? The clock is ticking for IREN as it aims to reposition itself as an AI infrastructure player, right when hyperscalers \u2014 those sprawling cloud companies \u2014 are in a mad dash for electricity, semiconductors, and real estate. On Thursday, Applied Digital disclosed a $7.5 billion, 15-year lease deal with an undisclosed U.S. hyperscaler. According to Reuters Breakingviews, Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet, and Meta could collectively lay out close to $630 billion on data centers and AI chips by 2026. Three commentary pieces in the last two days highlighted sharply divided opinions on IREN. Seeking Alpha flagged the risk that a hefty share-sale \u2014 potentially $6 billion \u2014 might undermine the company\u2019s push into hyperscale. Simply Wall St pointed to analyst skepticism about transforming crypto infrastructure for AI use. Yahoo Finance circled back to IREN following a notable run-up in the stock. IREN on March 4 said it struck a deal to acquire over 50,000 Nvidia B300 GPUs\u2014these are the chips powering AI model training and operation. With that batch, IREN\u2019s targeted GPU capacity jumps to 150,000. Management is projecting the expanded fleet could back more than $3.7 billion in annualized AI cloud revenue by late 2026, based on forecasted pricing and total installed units. The company also noted it had locked in $9.3 billion in funding across the past eight months. Microsoft inked a $9.7 billion deal for cloud services back in November, spelling out a five-year arrangement with deployments rolling out at IREN\u2019s Childress, Texas campus through 2026. By the end of January, IREN counted $2.8 billion in cash and cash equivalents and said it had more than 4.5 gigawatts of power capacity on the grid, boosted by a 1.6-gigawatt addition in Oklahoma. Even so, the company\u2019s latest numbers highlight just how much of the transition is still ahead. For the quarter ending Dec. 31, IREN pulled in $167.4 million from bitcoin mining, while AI Cloud Services brought in just $17.3 million. Back then, co-founder and co-CEO Daniel Roberts described the market as the company\u2019s \u201cstrongest demand environment to date,\u201d but AI was still far from dominating the top line. Nasdaq Back in March, Roberts was clear: \u201cScaling to 150,000 GPUs\u201d could make IREN one of the world\u2019s top AI cloud infrastructure players. According to the company, those additional chips are set to roll out in phases through the latter half"

Stock Market Today, April 23: Iren Jumps on Ongoing Data Center Pivot Motley Fool - Thu Apr 23, 4:34PM CDT Iren (NASDAQ:IREN) , a Bitcoin mining and AI data center operator, closed Thursday at $52.02, up 7.50%. The stock advanced on positive coverage of its data center pivot that highlighted its growth potential. Trading volume reached 57.2 million shares, coming in about 56% above its three-month average of 36.7 million shares. Iren IPO'd in 2021 and has grown 86% since going public. How the markets moved today The S&P 500 (SNPINDEX:^GSPC) slipped 0.41% to finish Thursday at 7,108, while the Nasdaq Composite (NASDAQINDEX:^IXIC) fell 0.89% to close at 24,439. Within data processing services, industry peers Mara Holdings (NASDAQ:MARA) fell 0.34% to close at $11.80, and Riot Platforms (NASDAQ:RIOT) dropped 1.46% to finish at $18.21, lagging Iren\u2019s strong gains. What this means for investors Iren is one of several Bitcoin mining companies to transition into providing energy for artificial intelligence (AI) and data centers. It has soared over 750% in the past year on growing demand for AI computing capacity, including a deal with Microsoft in November. Today\u2019s gains extend its upward momentum, following reports that some analysts view it as undervalued. It will report Q3 earnings on May 7, when investors will be looking for details on a possible $6 billion share issuance. The funds would further cement its transition away from Bitcoin and into AI infrastructure, but some are concerned about share dilution and want clarity on revenue projections. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 20, 2026 Iren Expands AI Data Center Capacity to 4.5 Gigawatts According to a report from Yahoo Finance , Iren Limited has significantly expanded its portfolio of power-connected land for data centers. The company, noted for its vertically integrated model of owning both land and facilities, recently secured an additional 1.6 gigawatts of grid-connected property in Oklahoma . This acquisition complements its existing 160 megawatts of capacity in Canada and a 2.75 gigawatt footprint across two sites in Texas, bringing its total potential capacity to 4.5 gigawatts. The company's strategic shift from Bitcoin mining to providing computing for artificial intelligence applications has coincided with a substantial increase in its stock price over the preceding twelve months. Despite this rise, the stock remains below its historical peak. A key factor in its recent performance is a major agreement with Microsoft , which is projected to generate nearly $2 billion in annualized revenue from 200 megawatts of capacity at high project-level margins. Based on the financial terms of that agreement, analysts project that fully utilizing the entire 4.5 gigawatt portfolio could result in annual revenue exceeding $40 billion. Should the margin structure from the Microsoft deal hold, the associated adjusted EBITDA could approach $37 billion, before accounting for corporate expenses. Currently, the company has projected annual recurring revenue for the year from just over ten percent of its total capacity. 1. IREN may avoid the main bottleneck for US-based AI data centers Share link: In this post: IREN may lead the AI data center game with access to 2.74 GW in easily available power. Short open interest spiked on IREN shares in the short term, but the shares retain their bullish sentiment. IREN leads the drive for data centers, where other companies face delays due to an energy access bottleneck. IREN may win the data center game, based on its available energy contracts from the era of Bitcoin mining. The company has a head start in building data centers, where access to reliable energy is turning into a key bottleneck. Iren Limited (Nasdaq:IREN) recently drew attention to BTC mining stocks and may signal a shift in general sentiment for mining companies. Traditionally, IREN has tracked the price of BTC and the overall crypto sentiment. As of April 2026, IREN has entered a key pivot stage, when the stock may start to reflect the AI narrative, while depending less on tracking the crypto market. IREN traded at around $45.12, bouncing from the year\u2019s lows at $31.62. IREN recovered in April, drawing attention to its significant energy contract portfolio. | Source: Google Finance IREN is closely watched as the most active Bitcoin mining stock, which may lead to recoveries in other assets. Most of the leading Bitcoin mining stocks are in the green for the year to date, based on recent market performance data . IREN still attracts short open interest IREN has seen regular spikes in short open interest. As of April 22, short open interest is at 18.42% of the stock\u2019s free float. IREN short positions are still small compared to the short open interest for Mara Holdings (Nasdaq: MARA) at up to 30%, as well as CleanSpark (Nasdaq: CLSK) . Despite this, traders aim to grab the opportunity for shorting IREN, benefiting from daily swings. In this round, Nebius continued to demonstrate strong results for AI inference workloads running on the latest NVIDIA platforms. Our submission includes systems built on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra GPUs \u2014 NVIDIA HGX B200, NVIDIA HGX B300 and the rack-scale NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 system. We benchmarked three models in this round: DeepSeek R1, Qwen3-VL 235B and gpt-oss 120B. Two of these models, Qwen3-VL 235B, a multimodal model capable of understanding both visual and text inputs; and gpt-oss 120B, a large open-source language model, were introduced to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference benchmark suite in this release. Together with DeepSeek R1, these benchmarks represent the type of frontier-scale models increasingly used in modern AI applications. The MLPerf\u00ae benchmark suite continues to evolve alongside the AI industry, introducing new workloads that reflect the latest trends in large language and multimodal models. Our results in this round demonstrate Nebius\u2019 readiness to run these frontier-scale workloads on cutting-edge AI infrastructure. Ready for frontier model inference Ready for frontier model inference In the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 round, Nebius benchmarked three NVIDIA AI systems built on the latest Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra architectures: NVIDIA HGX B200 NVIDIA HGX B300 NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 Overall, Nebius achieved 10 first-place results out of 16 benchmark submissions, along with other vendors submitting results. We also submitted five additional use cases where we were the only submitter, so we do not count them as first-place results. TD Synnex has reserved over 1,000 high-end Nvidia GPUs in the cloud to help solution providers overcome supply constraints and get their customers moving on AI projects. The U.S. distribution giant, which won Nvidia\u2019s Distribution Partner of the Year award for the Americas last month , made the cloud commitment for AI computing clusters based on Nvidia\u2019s B300 GPU with neocloud provider Nebius. TD Synnex announced the deal on Tuesday at its High-Growth Conference in Puerto Rico. [Related: Nvidia Channel Chief: Neoclouds Represent \u2018Next Phase Of Opportunity\u2019 For Partners ] The Fremont, Calif.-based company is accepting purchase orders for B300-based instances through Nebius now, with availability expected to begin in July. The agreement lasts for a year, and it gives TD Synnex the option for renewal. Nvidia Americas channel chief Craig Weinstein told CRN last month that the company sees the partnership as an important way to reach enterprise customers. \u201cIt means that we have an [Nvidia Cloud Partner] that\u2019s going to offer cloud capability to the ecosystem to ensure [that] enterprises can iterate and solution providers have a platform to leverage,\u201d he said. TD Synnex-Nebius Deal Seen As Way To Combat Supply Scarcity In an exclusive interview with CRN, TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato said that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to what has become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. \u201cThere\u2019s literally no capacity available right now,\u201d said Criado, senior vice president of cloud, security and AI at TD Synnex. TD Synnex expects the Nebius deal to benefit many of its strategic partners who otherwise \u201cwouldn\u2019t get the prioritization that they would need to gain access to the GPUs,\u201d according to Donato, senior director AI and neocloud vendor solutions at TD Synnex. \u201cOur ability to give them that prioritization up there with the general hyperscalers and neocloud providers just gives them access, in general,\u201d he said. \u201cCould they get the same capacity direct? Maybe, but there\u2019s no availability.\u201d TD Synnex: Deal Represents First Of Its Kind In Disty World According to TD Synnex, the Nebius engagement \u201cmarks the first time a global IT distributor has reserved a dedicated Nvidia AI factory-grade cluster from an AI-native cloud provider.\u201d \u201cIt\u2019s not your typical distribution go-to-market where you\u2019re buying and reselling something,\u201d Criado said. \u201cWe\u2019re actually committing and we\u2019ve purchased this reserved instance within the Nebius data center ... and we will now work with our partners and their customers to [drive consumption].\u201d Based on Nvidia\u2019s Blackwell Ultra architecture, the B300 debuted last year as the company\u2019s flagship GPU for x86-based servers that"

Calcalist previously reported that the Israeli startup had held advanced discussions with Nvidia at a reported valuation of $2 billion to $3 billion, above its last known valuation of $1.4 billion from 2023. Those talks reached senior levels but ultimately did not materialize into a transaction. 1 View gallery AI21 Labs founders. ( Photo: Orel Cohen ) In January, co-founder and chairman Amnon Shashua confirmed that AI21 was engaged in discussions with Nvidia as well as other potential buyers, indicating that the company was formally exploring strategic options. Founded in 2017 by Shashua, Professor Yoav Shoham and Ori Goshen, AI21 was among the early companies developing large-scale language models. However, it has struggled in recent years to keep pace with leading AI developers such as OpenAI and Anthropic. The company has since narrowed its focus to enterprise applications, emphasizing accuracy and reliability. It discontinued development of its consumer product, Wordtune, and now centers on offerings such as its Maestro platform and a recently introduced reasoning model. Annual revenue is estimated at around $50 million. Related articles: \u201cThere are talks with Nvidia and others\u201d: Shashua confirms AI21 acquisition discussions AI21 never closed its reported $300 million round as Nvidia weighs an acquisition Nvidia in advanced talks to acquire AI21 in $2-3 billion deal focused on talent AI21 employs roughly 200 people, down from about 260 in 2023, after scaling back expansion plans. Much of the interest in the company has been attributed to its workforce, which includes highly trained AI researchers. The earlier Nvidia talks were widely viewed as driven primarily by this talent base, rather than by AI21\u2019s commercial scale. Nebius, by contrast, has been expanding its presence in enterprise AI infrastructure. Earlier this year, it acquired Israeli startup Tavily for up"

It is a way of translating long term cash flow expectations into a single present value per share. For Nebius Group, the model uses a 2 Stage Free Cash Flow to Equity approach based on cash flow projections in US$. The latest twelve month free cash flow is a loss of $3,545.57 million. Analyst estimates and extrapolations then feed into ten year projections that move from further free cash flow losses in the near term to a projected free cash flow of $4,780 million in 2030, with additional extrapolated figures through 2035. Bringing all of those projected cash flows back to today gives an estimated intrinsic value of US$627.77 per share. Compared to the current share price of US$154.56, the DCF output suggests that Nebius Group is about 75.4% undervalued on this model. The partnership introduces production-grade NVIDIA AI factory-grade GPU clusters to TD SYNNEX's worldwide channel. Nebius Group, listed as NasdaqGS:NBIS, becomes the first AI native cloud provider to supply this type of dedicated, globally distributed GPU capacity through a major IT distributor. For Nebius Group, this move puts its AI focused cloud infrastructure directly into the hands of TD SYNNEX's partner network, which serves a broad base of enterprise customers. Instead of only relying on large hyperscaler contracts, Nebius is now positioned as an AI infrastructure option that channel partners can package into their own solutions for clients that want predictable, production ready compute. For investors, this collaboration illustrates how Nebius Group is being incorporated into the broader enterprise technology stack rather than being limited to the largest technology companies. The scale and structure of this reseller GPU cluster help show how AI workloads are being sourced and standardized across the channel, and how Nebius is seeking to participate in that spending over time. Micah Zimmerman, The Motley Fool Mon, April 20, 2026 at 12:42 AM UTC 0 Key Points An up to $50 billion contracted backlog with Microsoft and Meta Platforms gives Nebius rare visibility into future revenue. The company is spending big and risks can't be ignored. 10 stocks we like better than Nebius Group \u203a There's a version of the AI infrastructure story that almost everyone knows: Nvidia (NASDAQ: NVDA) makes the chips, the hyperscalers build the data centers, and the software companies build on top. But the middle layer in that stack -- which is the specialized cloud providers who rent GPU compute to companies building artificial intelligence (AI) models -- has one company with a contracted backlog so large relative to its current size that it's hard not to stop and look. Nebius Group (NASDAQ: NBIS) is not a traditional start-up. It emerged in 2024 from the restructuring of Yandex , the Russian internet giant. Its founding CEO, Arkady Volozh -- who built Yandex into a company handling billions of search queries -- walked into this venture with hundreds of experienced infrastructure engineers, $2.5 billion in capital, and decades of experience building and operating large-scale data center systems. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland. The new facility will be one of Europe's largest when completed, the company said. In this article NBIS Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland, which it says will be one of Europe's largest when up-and-running, as the region races to develop the compute infrastructure needed to power the AI boom. The new facility will be based in the Finnish city of Lappeenranta with a capacity of up to 310 MW. Nebius said it expects the data center to begin initially supplying customers by 2027. \"We have been building in Finland for many years and are pleased to be expanding our presence here,\" said Nebius CEO Arkady Volozh in a statement. \"Lappeenranta represents a significant addition to our global AI infrastructure build-out, and will make a significant contribution to achieving our capacity goals.\" Europe's AI buildout It comes amid a slew of AI infrastructure announcements across Europe. French AI startup Mistral said Monday it had secured $830 million in debt financing to operate a data center near Paris. That came after a February announcement of a 1.2-billion-euro ($1.38 billion) plan to build data centers and compute capacity in Sweden. U.K. startup Nscale announced it had raised $2 billion at a $14.6 billion valuation earlier this month, with plans to develop AI data centers in its home market alongside Europe and the U.S. Meanwhile, 2025 saw MGX, Bpifrance, Mistral and Nvidia unveil plans for a 1.4 GW AI campus in France and Brookfield said it"

preferred source on Google BIRMINGHAM, Ala. ( WBRC ) - New developments are emerging in a plan to build a massive \u201cAI factory\u201d in Birmingham\u2019s Oxmoor Valley neighborhood, as city officials clarify that critical portions of the project can move forward without additional zoning approvals. The update follows an hourslong meeting before the city\u2019s Zoning Board of Adjustment last month, where the proposal sparked intense discussion and community concern. In the weeks since, the city has determined that Alabama Power and Nebius do not need special exceptions to build a substation and a switching station tied to the project. Nebius, a Netherlands-based tech company, owns about 80 acres off Lakeshore Parkway. They ae hoping to build a two-building AI factory in a former bank-back office building. (WBRC) The clarification comes after confusion surrounding actions taken during the March 26 Zoning Board of Adjustment meeting. At that time, the board appeared to recommend approval for a substation connected to the proposed Nebius AI"

AI developers and engineers are being forced into a trade-off that shouldn\u2019t exist: move fast with managed AI services and give up control, or build on raw infrastructure and absorb the full cost of complexity. \u200d This is becoming one of the defining bottlenecks of enterprise AI adoption. \u200d Enterprise spending on generative AI has more than tripled year-over-year , while token usage and inference demand are growing at unprecedented rates. Gemini alone saw token usage increase 50x and inference is expected to account for more than half of total AI compute by 2030, according to McKinsey. At the same time, studies from firms like Deloitte consistently point to the same constraint: the gap between experimentation and production remains the hardest part of deploying AI at scale. \u200d Teams can prototype quickly, but turning those prototypes into reliable, cost-efficient, and governed systems is still slow, expensive, and operationally complex. For enterprises, that challenge is compounded by the need to meet sovereignty and compliance requirements without sacrificing performance. \u200d The result is a structural mismatch. Model capability is accelerating. Enterprise infrastructure and enterprise tooling are not keeping pace, while sovereignty and compliance concerns continue to grow. \u200d For engineering teams, that mismatch shows up in very real ways: Environments designed for CPU workloads that are not cost effective for GPU workloads Cost of hardware and rate of change mean that they need to compromise in performance and even worse, the value they get out of"

    "content": "BT Launches Sovereign Cloud & AI Stack with Nscale & NVIDIA to Strengthen UK Digital Sovereignty Technologies Services Business Devices Home Blog Insights Awards and lists Events Directory MWC26 Special Edition: GenAI 2026 Trends Podcasts BT Launches Sovereign Cloud & AI Stack with Nscale & NVIDIA to Strengthen UK Digital Sovereignty Friday, 24 April 2026 00:38 Ray Sharma 1 2 3 4 5 (0 votes) 3.2 min read font size decrease font size increase font size Comment Image Credit: mkabakov/Bigstockphoto.com BT Business announced the UK\u2019s first full suite of sovereign services, offering public and private sector organisations an end-to-end portfolio spanning sovereign connectivity, voice, cloud and AI. The expanded sovereign platform is built to help customers keep sensitive workloads in the UK, strengthen operational control and deploy advanced digital services faster \u2013 without compromising on security, resilience or compliance. The commercial launch comes as a new report from Assembly Research finds that concerns over data security are holding back AI adoption in the UK. By giving organisations clearer control over how data is stored, accessed and governed, digital sovereignty can provide the confidence needed to scale AI securely \u2013 unlocking an estimated \u00a318bn productivity boost for the UK economy. The report also highlights the wider commercial opportunity created by sovereign infrastructure. Accelerated investment in UK-based data centres could generate \u00a314.6bn by 2030, while the expansion of sovereign cloud services is estimated to be worth an additional \u00a313.6bn over the next five years. As part of the expanded sovereign portfolio, BT is building sovereign AI capability with Nscale and NVIDIA to deliver new sovereign AI solutions in the UK. This will enable organisations to run AI workloads domestically, scale capacity on demand and meet data residency, security and regulatory requirements \u2013 supporting use cases from operational automation to advanced analytics and AI-assisted customer service. BT is also launching Sovereign Cloud \u2013 a private cloud platform hosted and operated entirely within the UK. Designed for organisations handling sensitive or regulated workloads, it provides compute, storage and backup capabilities underpinned by Rackspace Technology\u2019s UK data centre infrastructure, with UK-based, security-cleared teams and managed services to support migration, operations and ongoing compliance. Jon James, Chief Executive Officer of BT Business Organisations, public and private, want to move fast with AI and cloud while keeping control over the sovereignty of their data. That\u2019s why BT is the first UK provider to offer a complete sovereign portfolio \u2013 from secure connectivity and voice to sovereign cloud and AI \u2013 all delivered in one place. Only BT has the scale and infrastructure to help customers modernise critical services with confidence, delivering real benefits for organisations and for the UK as a whole. Matthew Howett, Founder and Chief Executive of Assembly Research Our research shows that digital sovereignty has become a political focus across Europe and in the UK, as concerns about an over-reliance on non-sovereign digital platforms have intensified. We\u2019ll touch on more details about the campus later, but construction is expected to start soon with operations beginning in H2 2027. That shift is colliding with a hard physical reality: the compute behind modern AI runs hot, dense, power-hungry\u2014and remains scarce. It\u2019s pushing data center operators to deliver far more capacity, far faster, often with projects measured in the hundreds of megawatts and timelines that feel closer to months than years. Related insights Perspectives from the Global Telecom Outlook, 2025\u20132029 by Florian Gr\u00f6ne Industrial redevelopment offers an accelerated path for building data centers by Markus Sch\u00f6rghuber and Folker Trepte How to fund the data-center boom? The result is a new infrastructure arms race, with \u2018neoclouds\u2019 emerging alongside hyperscalers to deliver AI-first capacity. And that arms race is attracting massive amounts of capital. Data center investment has surged into the hundreds of billions of dollars annually, with PwC estimating that meeting capacity requirements will require about US$2 trillion in capex by 2030. The financing playbook is evolving in kind. New deal structures are emerging, blurring the lines between customer contracts, infrastructure finance, and hardware supply\u2014and raising new questions about risk sharing and resilience. Nidhi CHAPPELL has had a front-row seat to how these dynamics are reshaping strategy. She currently serves in the C-suite at Europe-based neocloud Nscale as Global President of AI Infrastructure, and previously held a senior AI infrastructure leadership role at Microsoft Azure. And to underpin its service provisioning plans, it has announced that it will, in partnership with UK-based neocloud Nscale, develop \u201csovereign AI datacentres in the UK, using Nvidia full stack AI infrastructure\u201d. The national UK telco stated: \u201cThe move will expand the UK\u2019s AI capability using trusted nationwide networks \u2013 enabling organisations to adopt AI securely, at scale and under UK control. Nscale plans to build up to 14 megawatts of AI datacentre infrastructure across three existing strategic BT sites in the UK. BT will provide infrastructure and connectivity to these new datacentres, which will significantly expand the UK\u2019s AI compute capacity in response to surging demand.\u200d\u201d This deployment \u201cwill enable organisations to run AI workloads domestically, scale capacity on demand and meet data residency, security and regulatory requirements \u2013 supporting use cases from operational automation to advanced analytics and AI-assisted customer service.\u201d Organisations using BT\u2019s sovereign AI capability will be able to: Run AI models in the UK, supporting regulatory, data residency and security requirements Scale AI capacity on demand, without upfront capital investment in infrastructure Adopt advanced AI technologies, while maintaining operational resilience and control More on this plan in a moment\u2026 With regards to the services, BT announced in December last year that it was to launch a new sovereign data platform and kickstarted its offer with the unveiling in late February of Sovereign Voice , a \u201cnew state-of-the-art sovereign cloud calling system that offers"

The facility is planned for the Sievari industrial area, owned by the Town of Harjavalta. Fortum is supporting the project as site development partner. \u200d The project is inline with Fortum\u2019s strategic priorities to drive electrification in theNordics. Fortum laid the groundwork for this site development in June 2024 by signing a planning reservation agreement with the Town of Harjavalta. \u200d The agreement covered marketing, zoning, and grid connection support for a potential data center site in Sievari and marked the first phase of advancing the area for future investment. \u200d Fortum has now signed a site securing and development agreement (SSDA) with Nscale and will continue the work initiated in 2024. In parallel, Nscale and the Town of Harjavalta have begun the process toward a preliminary land sale of the Sievari site. Under the agreement, Fortum\u2019s role is to support the smooth progress of Nscale\u2019s project development, including grid connection design and permitting. \u200d The total power capacity of the planned data center is to be determined during the next design and permitting phases. \u200d \u201cThis cooperation reflects our broader strategy to enable low-carbon growth by providing customers with the right conditions to build scalable, sustainable operations in Finland. These types of projects play a key role in driving electrification and digitalisation in our societies,\u201d Jyrki Holappa , Customer Site Development Director at Fortum states. \u200d \u201cThe Nordic market, and Finland specifically, offers unique advantages that align perfectly with our"

The initiative focuses on expanding domestic compute capacity while ensuring data stays within national borders. It brings together BT\u2019s estate and connectivity with Nscale\u2019s modular approach to data centre development. Using NVIDIA \u2019s full stack AI platform, the partners aim to support organisations that require high-performance computing without building their own facilities. Nscale plans to deliver up to 14MW of AI data centre capacity across three existing BT sites. BT will provide the underlying infrastructure, including power and network integration, which together form the foundation for scalable AI workloads. Building sovereign AI capacity The development sits within BT Business\u2019 sovereign platform, which allows organisations to run AI workloads entirely within the UK. Hosting compute resources locally means that organisations can meet compliance standards while still accessing advanced processing power. Jon James , CEO, BT Business, says: \u201cThe development of AI and data centre infrastructure is key to unlocking economic growth in the UK, and is only possible with the best, most trusted networks at the foundation. \u201cDigital sovereignty enables UK organisations to adopt the world\u2019s leading technologies, while maintaining resilience and control in a rapidly changing world. \u201cOur collaboration with Nscale reflects BT\u2019s unique position as the digital backbone of the UK \u2013 the only provider with the scale, capabilities and experience to enable the nation\u2019s sovereign ambitions.\u201d Jon James, CEO of BT says AI and data centre infrastructure is key for unlocking the UK's economic growth The facilities are designed to support a range of workloads, from analysing datasets to enabling AI-led applications in sectors such as energy and security. Policy alignment and infrastructure demand The investment aligns with the UK Government\u2019s AI Opportunities Action Plan , which prioritises domestic infrastructure as a means of supporting economic activity and technological capability. Data centres form a core part of this strategy, providing the compute layer required for AI development and deployment.     "content": "Nvidia-Backed Nscale and BT Partner to Expand 14MW AI Data Centre Capacity in the UK - CXO Digitalpulse Home CXO pulse LEADERSHIP TALKS TECH TALKS BUDGET 2026-27 CXO TV Startup Pulse Content Hub Advertorials News/Media Infographics Whitepaper / Reports Events Newsletters Submit your Article Search CXO Musings - CXO Digitalpulse CXO Musings - CXO Digitalpulse CXO Musings - CXO Digitalpulse Home CXO pulse LEADERSHIP TALKS TECH TALKS BUDGET 2026-27 CXO TV Startup Pulse Content Hub Advertorials News/Media Infographics Whitepaper / Reports Events Newsletters Submit your Article Facebook Linkedin Twitter Youtube Search Home CXO pulse LEADERSHIP TALKS TECH TALKS BUDGET 2026-27 CXO TV Startup Pulse Content Hub Advertorials News/Media Infographics Whitepaper / Reports Events Newsletters Submit your Article Search Home News/Media Nvidia-Backed Nscale and BT Partner to Expand 14MW AI Data Centre Capacity in the UK April 24, 2026 0 139 Facebook Twitter Pinterest WhatsApp Linkedin Nvidia-backed AI infrastructure company Nscale has partnered with British telecom giant BT to expand artificial intelligence data centre capacity in the UK, marking a significant step in the country\u2019s push toward sovereign AI infrastructure. The collaboration will see the deployment of up to 14 megawatts of AI data centre capacity across three existing BT sites. The initiative is designed to support both public and private sector organizations by providing access to advanced AI computing resources within the UK. By leveraging Nvidia-powered infrastructure, the project aims to deliver high-performance computing capabilities while ensuring data remains within national borders, addressing growing concerns around data security and regulatory compliance. BT will contribute its nationwide network infrastructure and connectivity, forming the backbone of the new AI facilities. Meanwhile, Nscale will develop and operate the data centres, using its modular, scalable approach to AI infrastructure. This partnership reflects a broader trend where telecom companies and AI specialists collaborate to deliver integrated, high-capacity computing solutions. The expansion builds on BT\u2019s sovereign data platform, launched to ensure that sensitive data can be stored and processed domestically. With increasing demand for secure and resilient digital infrastructure, the platform enables organizations to run AI workloads locally while meeting strict data residency and compliance requirements. Nscale, founded in 2024 and backed by Nvidia, has been rapidly scaling its operations in the AI infrastructure space. The company recently raised $2 billion in funding, reaching a valuation of $14.6 billion, highlighting strong investor confidence in the growing demand for AI data centres. The project also aligns with the UK government\u2019s broader strategy to strengthen domestic AI capabilities and reduce reliance on foreign-controlled infrastructure. Officials have emphasized that investments in AI data centres are critical to enabling businesses and public services to adopt AI at scale while supporting economic growth and technological independence. Overall, the partnership between Nscale and BT underscores the rising importance of sovereign AI infrastructure in the global technology landscape. As demand for AI computing continues to surge, such collaborations are expected to play a key role in building secure, scalable, and locally controlled AI ecosystems. - Advertisement - Previous article Tencent Unveils Hunyuan Hy3"

Kimi K2.5, the 1-trillion-parameter open-weight model that turns screenshots into code, coordinates 100 parallel agents, and outperforms leading closed models on key agentic benchmarks is now available on Nscale Inference Endpoints . \u200d Try Kimi K2.5 on Nscale \u2192 What sets Kimi K2.5 apart Kimi K2.5 from Moonshot AI is an open-weight, natively multimodal model with several architectural choices that set it apart. \u200d It uses a Mixture-of-Experts architecture with 1 trillion total parameters, selectively activating only a subset per request to improve efficiency. The result is frontier-level capability with efficient inference, without compromising performance on real workloads. \u200d Unlike models where vision was added later, Kimi K2.5 was trained from the ground up on 15 trillion mixed visual and text tokens. Images and text are processed as a single unified stream rather than separate pipelines, enabling stronger cross-modal reasoning rather than simple image captioning. \u200d Developers get direct control over model behaviour with thinking mode on Nscale, enabling deeper, multi-step reasoning when needed, with per-request control over the latency and quality trade-off. \u200d Kimi K2.5 in practice Kimi K2.5\u2019s combination of native vision, long-context reasoning, and strong coding performance makes it well suited to workflows where inputs are messy, tasks are multi-step, and outputs need to be production-ready. \u200d Three areas where it stands out below. 1. Design-to-code and visual development What it does: Kimi K2.5 takes a screenshot or mockup and generates fully functional front-end code, including dynamic layouts, scroll"

David Chernicoff , Matt Vincent April 3, 2026 15 min read Key Highlights Nscale has secured over $2 billion in funding and is valued at approximately $14.6 billion, reflecting strong investor confidence in its integrated AI infrastructure approach. The company's acquisition of AIPCorp enables it to develop large-scale energy microgrids, such as the Monarch Compute Campus, designed to deliver up to 8 gigawatts of power, bypassing utility constraints. Nscale is establishing a multi-regional campus network, including sites in the US, UK, Norway, Portugal, and Finland, each tailored to regional energy, regulatory, and political advantages. Partnerships with industry leaders like Microsoft and Nvidia are central to Nscale\u2019s strategy, ensuring demand-driven development aligned with next-generation AI hardware and software platforms. The company's focus on energy certainty, through on-site generation and microgrids, aims to reduce deployment delays and create a reliable supply chain for hyperscale AI workloads. Fidelisinfra.com Rendering of the Monarch Compute Campus Nscale has moved quickly from startup to serious contender in the race to build infrastructure for the AI era. Founded in 2024, the company has positioned itself as a vertically integrated \u201cneocloud\u201d operator , combining data center development, GPU fleet ownership, and a software stack designed to deliver large-scale AI compute. That model has helped it attract backing from investors including Nvidia, and in early March 2026 the company raised another $2 billion at a reported $14.6 billion valuation. Reuters has described Nscale\u2019s approach as owning and operating its own data centers, GPUs, and software stack to support major customers including Microsoft and OpenAI. What makes Nscale especially relevant now is that it is no longer content to operate as a cloud intermediary or capacity provider. Over the past year, the company has increasingly framed itself as an AI hyperscaler and AI factory builder, seeking to combine land, power, data center shells, GPU procurement, customer offtake, and software services into a single integrated platform. Its acquisition of American Intelligence & Power Corporation, or AIPCorp , is the clearest signal yet of that shift, bringing energy infrastructure directly into the center of Nscale\u2019s business model. The AIPCorp transaction is significant because it gives Nscale more than"

The meeting also provided the public with an opportunity to interact with Nscale representatives and local leaders to address their concerns with the project.     "content": "Microsoft is embracing off-grid gas for West Virginia data centers | Latitude Media Microsoft is embracing off-grid gas for West Virginia data centers | Latitude Media Subscribe Covering the new frontiers of the energy transition Tech Grid edge Solar Energy storage Artificial intelligence Data centers Carbon removal Hydrogen Emerging tech Geothermal Nuclear See all Resilience Markets U.S. market Global markets Policy Regulation Research See all Deals Venture capital Project finance Corporates Mergers and acquisitions Government funding See all Tech Grid edge Solar Energy storage Artificial intelligence Data centers Carbon removal Hydrogen Emerging tech Geothermal Nuclear See all Resilience Markets U.S. market Global markets Policy Regulation Research See all Deals Venture capital Project finance Corporates Mergers and acquisitions Government funding See all Podcasts Events Research Resources Latitude Studios About Newsletter Authors Staff Careers Advertise Contact Tech Markets Deals Podcasts Events Research Resources Analysis Corporates DATA CENTERS Microsoft is embracing off-grid gas for West Virginia data centers The hyperscaler's deal with \"neocloud\" developer Nscale takes advantage of a new state law to bypass grid delays. Catherine Boudreau April 1, 2026 Add Latitude on Google Photo credit: Aerovista Luchtfotografie / Shutterstock West Virginia, with its ready supply of cheap gas and a new law allowing off-grid data centers, may be the next frontier for big-name customers like Microsoft. The hyperscaler in March signed a \u201cletter of intent\u201d to secure nearly 1.4 gigawatts of power from a microgrid project underway in Mason County, W. Va. The developer, Nscale \u2014 a two-year-old startup building AI compute campuses in the U.S. and Europe \u2014 said it plans to deploy hundreds of gas generators by the first half of 2028. The deal marks the first time that Microsoft has committed to a fully off-grid, gas-powered data center at the gigawatt scale, said Olivia Wang, a research analyst at Sightline who\u2019s tracking the global data center pipeline . Previously, she added, the company has mainly used gas generators for backup capacity in new data center builds, not as a primary energy source. Nscale took over the project when it acquired American Intelligence & Power Corporation, which specialized in behind-the-meter microgrids for AI data centers. Stuart Pann, senior advisor at Nscale, told Latitude Media in an interview at CERAWeek that the company saw a lot of opportunity in West Virginia because it sits atop the Marcellus Formation, the largest gas field in the country that spans Appalachia. \u201cWe\u2019re taking advantage of the geographic location of this facility, which can have four separate pipelines fed into it. There\u2019s a chance to make this a gas hub, in the same way Henry Hub is,\u201d Pann said, referring to a gas pipeline interchange in Louisiana that serves as a price benchmark for North America. A Microsoft spokesperson said the company takes a portfolio approach to energy for its global AI infrastructure based on local conditions, grid readiness, and long\u2011term goals. That flexibility allows Microsoft to \u201cbring capacity online faster, ensure reliability, and limit strain on local grids while continuing to invest in"
